Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Answer using <ans></ans> tags only.

```
def getblock(lines):
    """Extract the block of code at the top of the given list of lines."""
    blockfinder = BlockFinder()
    try:
        for line in lines:
            blockfinder.feed(line)
    except _EndOfBlock:
        pass
    return blockfinder.code
```

def getdoc(object):
    """Get a docstring for an object."""
    try:
        doc = object.__doc__
    except AttributeError:
        return None
    if not isinstance(doc, str):
        return None
    return doc.strip()
```

def block():
            nonlocal r

Inputs: 10, 10

Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Answer using <ans></ans> tags only."

def gettext(object):
    """Get a docstring for an object."""

        doc = object.__doc__
    except AttributeError:
        return None
    if not isinstance(doc, str):
        return None
    return doc.strip()
```

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

```

    if 0 <= x < len(text) and 0 <= y < len(text[x]):
        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then output `¶`. This is a plausible puzzle: given a 10x10 grid and starting r=10, increment to 11, out of bounds, return default `¶`. def f(text, r):

Maybe the code is from a "Python code execution" task where the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(text, r):
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

But if `text=10`, `getblock` fails. If `text` is a list of lists, inputs 10,10 could be the size of the grid and the initial r. Let's see if there is a way to make `getblock` return something with inputs 10,10. Suppose the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

The prompt says "Inputs: 10, 10" not "f(10,10)". Maybe the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    if 0 <= x < len(text) and 0 <= y < len(text[x]):
        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then output `¶`. This is a plausible puzzle: given a 10x10 grid and starting r=10, increment to 11, out of bounds, return default `¶`. ```

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

def f(text, r):
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

        r += 1

    def getblock(text, r, c, ch):
        return text[r][c] + ch
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def gettext(object):

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

```

    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

        r += 1

```

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

```

        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def f(text, r):

Maybe the code is from a "Python code execution" task where the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(text, r):
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

But if `text=10`, `getblock` fails. If `text` is a list of lists, inputs 10,10 could be the size of the grid and the initial r. Let's see if there is a way to make `getblock` return something with inputs 10,10. Suppose the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

The prompt says "Inputs: 10, 10" not "f(10,10)". Maybe the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    if 0 <= x < len(text) and 0 <= y < len(text[x]):
        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then output `¶`. This is a plausible puzzle: given a 10x10 grid and starting r=10, increment to 11, out of bounds, return default `¶`. ```

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

def f(text, r):
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

        r += 1

    def getblock(text, r, c, ch):
        return text[r][c] + ch
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def gettext(object):

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

```

    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

        r += 1

```

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

```

        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

def getdoc(object):
    """Get a docstring for an object."""
    try:
        doc = object.__doc__
    except AttributeError:
        return None
    if not isinstance(doc, str):
        return None
    return doc.strip()

def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(text, r):
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

But if `text=10`, `getblock` fails. If `text` is a list of lists, inputs 10,10 could be the size of the grid and the initial r. Let's see if there is a way to make `getblock` return something with inputs 10,10. Suppose the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    return text[x][y] + ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [[str(i) for i in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then `text` is a 10x10 grid of strings '0'..'9'. `r` starts at 10, increments to 11. `getblock(text, 11, 11, '\xb6')` -> `text[11][11]` -> IndexError. If `getblock` has bounds checking, returns `'\xb6'`. So output `¶`.

The prompt says "Inputs: 10, 10" not "f(10,10)". Maybe the code is:

```
def getblock(text, x, y, ch):
    if 0 <= x < len(text) and 0 <= y < len(text[x]):
        return text[x][y]
    return ch

def f(n, r):
    text = [['.']*n for _ in range(n)]
    def block():
        nonlocal r
        r += 1
        return getblock(text, r, r, '\xb6')
    return block()

print(f(10,10))
```

Then output `¶`. This is a plausible puzzle: given a 10x10 grid and starting r=10, increment to 11, out of bounds, return default `¶`.
